---
name: confirm-before-big-steps
description: Always confirm with the user before starting a big step such as dispatching agents, opening a new issue's work, or merging; do the requested change, explain it, then wait for the OK
metadata:
  type: feedback
---

Before any big step (dispatching subagents, starting the next issue, merging a PR,
rewriting history) stop, explain what was done and what comes next, and wait for an
explicit OK. Make the requested changes first, explain how they were made, then ask.

**Why:** On 2026-10-23 in parcel-rates the user interrupted me as I was about to apply
schema changes and dispatch parser agents in one go: "always confirm with me before
starting a next big step, like dispatching agents". They want to review each step.

**How to apply:** Routine edits, commits on a feature branch and tests run without asking.
Anything that spawns agents, spends large budgets, or changes shared state waits for
confirmation. See [[data-collection-fully-scripted]].
---
name: data-collection-fully-scripted
description: Data collection must always be fully scripted and reproducible without an LLM; agents' ad-hoc curl/WebFetch downloads are not acceptable as the collection method
metadata:
  type: feedback
---

Every step of data collection, including discovery of source URLs (e.g. Wayback CDX
lookups, file-name resolution), must live in committed scripts that run end to end
without an LLM or agent involved.

**Why:** On 2026-10-23 the user found that the parcel-rates survey agents had fetched
sources with one-off curl/WebFetch calls and only a post-hoc fetcher existed. Their rule:
the system must be independent of the agents used to build it, so the data can be
updated later or rebuilt on another computer without any LLM.

**How to apply:** When delegating data gathering, require the agent to deliver a script
(discovery + download + checksum manifest) rather than files alone; treat files without a
reproducing script as incomplete. Prefer polite sequential fetching with pauses.
See [[parcel-rates-project]].
- [Data collection fully scripted](data-collection-fully-scripted.md) — no LLM in the loop; discovery + download must be committed scripts
- [Confirm before big steps](confirm-before-big-steps.md) — do the change, explain, wait for OK before dispatching agents/merging
=== #28
docs: bring DECISIONS.md up to date with every decision made after day one [OPEN] docs
Review finding. `docs/DECISIONS.md` has three entries, all dated 2026-10-22. Every decision since then lives only in PR bodies, `docs/data/QUALITY.md` or the agent log. The brief asks for a record that connects major decisions to the conversation, and the interview asks for a decision changed after reviewing agent output.

**Add dated entries for at least:**
- Rate-card naming: UPS Daily Rates and FedEx Standard List Rates stored as `list`, not `retail`; USPS keeps `retail`/`commercial`, default commercial, with the reason.
- Schema amendments made during issue 2: `address_type` on surcharge rows, width threshold column, dimensional-weight rules with `effective_from`/`effective_to`, packaging-handling rule and the `nonstandard_packaging` parcel column, exceptions-as-warnings allowlist.

- Delivery area as a per-row opt-in instead of a ZIP lookup, and why.
- Sample mix reversed from a 71 %-at-a-loss mix to a typical home-goods mix with expedited `price_charged`.
- Base rates extended to 150 lb and minimum billable weight (issue 18).
- FedEx 2027 kept as a forward year with incomplete surcharge rules, flagged in the UI.
- Model switch mid-project (main session and subagents to Opus 5.5) to stay within usage limits.

**Add a short section "Decisions changed after reviewing agent output"** listing the zone-band correction, the rate-card renaming, the DAS toggle and the sample-mix reversal, each with a one-line pointer to the log or PR.

**Acceptance:** every PR merged after #12 is traceable to at least one entry; the "changed after review" section exists.
=== #29
tests: skip, not fail, the data tests that need PDFs when the PDFs are not fetched [OPEN] tests
Review finding. On a fresh clone without running `data/fetch_raw.py`, `uv run pytest data/tests` reports **600 failed, 268 passed**. Almost all failures are `Failed: <carrier>/<year>/<file>.pdf is missing; run python3 data/fetch_raw.py` from the citation, golden-cell and minimum-billable tests. The schema document says these tests skip with a message when the file has not been fetched; only two tests currently do.

**Change:**
- Any test that opens a raw PDF skips with the existing message when the file is absent.
- An environment flag (for example `PARCEL_RATES_REQUIRE_RAW=1`) turns those skips into failures for a full validation run and for CI once the fetch step runs.
- README "Tests" section states which suites need the PDFs and shows both commands.

**Acceptance:** fresh clone, no fetch: backend and data suites both green with skips reported; with the flag set and PDFs present: the same 838 checks pass as today.
=== #30
chore: Node requirement says 18.18+, but the unit tests need Node 20+ [OPEN] frontend
Review finding. `frontend/package.json` (`engines.node >=18.18`), `frontend/README.md` and the root README all state Node 18.18 or newer. On Node 18.18.2 `npx vitest run` fails immediately:

```
SyntaxError: The requested module 'node:util' does not provide an export named 'styleText'
```

`styleText` exists from Node 20.12 / 21.7. Playwright was deliberately pinned to 1.49 for Node 18, so the two tool choices contradict each other.

**Pick one:** state Node 20.12+ everywhere (engines, both READMEs) and unpin Playwright if desired, or pin vitest (and its dependencies) to a release that runs on Node 18. Docker is unaffected (image uses Node 22).

**Acceptance:** the documented Node version runs `npm ci && npm test && npm run build` without error.

# Decisions

Running log of the choices made for this project, in the order they were made.
Each entry says what was decided, the alternatives considered, and why.
See `docs/ASSIGNMENT.md` for the brief and `docs/agent-log/` for the agent
conversations that led here.

## 2026-10-22 — Problem, user and scope

**Problem.** A small online shop sends a few hundred to a few thousand parcels
a month, has no negotiated carrier contract, and pays the published list
prices of FedEx, UPS and the Postal Service. Today the carrier is chosen by
habit. The dashboard helps the owner choose the carrier per kind of parcel,
and check whether the flat shipping price charged to customers still covers
the real cost once surcharges and yearly price increases are included.

**Questions the dashboard answers.**

1. For a given parcel (weight, dimensions, distance, residential or business,
   required speed), which service is cheapest once surcharges are included,
   and what are the line items of that price?
2. Where are the breakeven points between the Postal Service and FedEx or UPS
   ground, by weight and distance?
3. For the owner's whole set of parcels, what is the blended cost per parcel
   with each carrier, and how much of it is surcharges rather than base rate?
4. How did that blended cost change year by year under each year's published
   prices, compared with the average increase the carriers announced, and
   which kinds of parcels in the mix drove the change?

**Scope decisions.**

- Timebox: issues are sized to fit about five hours of focused work (the
  brief's upper bound). Extras are tracked separately and only started if
  time remains. Time actually spent is recorded by the owner, not estimated.
- Services: three speed tiers across the three carriers — ground, two to
  three day, and next day. Weight capped at 70 lb (the USPS maximum).
- Years covered: decided after the data survey (issue 1), based on what can
  actually be downloaded and parsed reliably.
- Parcel input: CSV upload plus a bundled illustrative sample mix. Columns:
  weight, length, width, height, distance in miles, residential flag,
  required speed, and an optional per-parcel price charged to the customer.
  Dimensional weight is computed from the dimensions.
- Price charged to the customer: a single flat price is the MVP control. The
  optional per-parcel column overrides it when present.
- Fuel and peak surcharges: a documented per-year default taken from the
  published history, a slider to stress test the fuel percentage, and a peak
  season toggle. A next-year increase slider sits on the year-over-year view.
- Zone from distance: a documented distance-band to zone mapping. Real
  ZIP-to-ZIP zones are a bonus, not MVP.
- The app never fetches live data; all rate tables are committed.

## 2026-10-22 — Stack

| Area | Decision | Alternatives considered | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| Backend | FastAPI, Pydantic models, pandas over committed CSV tables loaded in memory, managed with `uv` | DuckDB; SQLite | Rate cards are a few thousand rows. No database keeps Docker and setup trivial. |
| Data collection | Python scripts under `data/` that download the carriers' published rate documents, parse them (pdfplumber for PDFs), and write normalized CSVs that are committed | Hand-typed tables | Reproducible, documents provenance, and qualifies for the scraping bonus. |
| Frontend | Vite + React + TypeScript, Recharts for line and bar charts, a custom SVG heatmap for the breakeven map, plain CSS with design tokens | Chart.js (needs a matrix plugin for heatmaps); Plotly (over 1 MB, poor on mobile); Tailwind | Small bundle, declarative, the heatmap is a trivial grid. |
| Docker | `docker compose` with two services: uvicorn backend and an nginx container serving the Vite build and proxying `/api` | Single multi-stage image with FastAPI serving static files | Clean separation, one command for the reviewer. |
| Tests | pytest for pipeline, rate engine and API; Vitest + Testing Library for components; Playwright end to end at 1440 and 375 px with screenshots reviewed for UX | — | Required by the brief and by us. |
| Workflow | Private GitHub repo, one issue per task, a branch and git worktree per issue, parallel agents each in their own worktree, pull requests merged with merge commits | Commit straight to main | Keeps history and lets agents work in parallel without stepping on each other. |
| Agent log | `SessionEnd` hook exports every session (and subagent) transcript to `docs/agent-log/<session-id>.md` and commits it; home directory path redacted | Manual export | Required by the brief, zero effort once set up. |

## 2026-10-22 — Year range and data policy (from the data survey, issue #1)

Full findings in `docs/data/QUALITY.md`; sources per file in `data/raw/<carrier>/SOURCES.md`.

- **Years: 2023 through 2026**, under each year's January list prices
  (USPS 2023 uses the July 2023 tables, when Ground Advantage launched).
  2022 is dropped from the MVP because USPS sold a different product set.
  FedEx 2027 rates are already published and are kept as an optional forward year.
- **Base rates are parsed** from USPS CSV zips, FedEx XLSX (2022, 2025–2027)
  and text PDFs (FedEx 2023–2024, UPS all years), each validated against
  hand-checked prices.
- **Surcharge rules are curated** into a CSV with a source file and page per
  row, rather than parsed from prose PDFs.
- **Fuel surcharge:** per-year default from FedEx's published history,
  applied to UPS as a proxy and flagged; the UI slider overrides it. USPS has none.
- **Announced increases:** FedEx and USPS from primary sources; UPS from
  trade press, marked secondary.
- **Raw files:** only spreadsheets, CSV zips and small HTML snapshots are
  committed (about 8 MB). All PDFs are gitignored and restored by
  `data/fetch_raw.py` from `data/raw/manifest.csv` (URL, SHA-256, size),
  fetched one at a time with a pause between requests.
- **Citations:** every curated row carries file, page and verbatim quote,
  and a test checks the quote against the document.
- **USPS dimensional weight** verified in the DMM: divisor 166, then 139 from
  2026-07-12, above 1,728 cubic inches, all zones.
- **Schema** for all processed tables and the parcel CSV is fixed in
  `docs/data/SCHEMA.md` before any parser is written.

=================== PR #15
Parse carrier rate tables into the schema (#2) 2026-10-23T09:30:55Z
## Summary
- `docs/data/SCHEMA.md`: contract for every processed table and the parcel CSV; rate cards (`retail`/`commercial` for USPS, `list` for UPS/FedEx), address type, width threshold, documented exceptions, golden corner cells, time-varying rules.
- `data/fetch_raw.py` + `data/raw/manifest.csv`: 135 sources with URLs and SHA-256; PDFs are not in git and are restored sequentially with pauses and backoff.
- `data/pipeline/{usps,ups,fedex}.py`: one command per carrier regenerates `data/processed/<carrier>/*.csv` from the raw files. Curated rules are Python literals with verbatim quotes, written to committed CSVs.
- Base rates: USPS 15,516 cells (2023–2026, two cards), UPS 9,800 (2023–2026), FedEx 14,700 (2023–2027).
- `docs/data/RULE_CHANGES.md`: every price-moving rule that changed between years.

## Tests
- Invariants (coverage, monotonicity, clean money and weight labels); anomalies must be documented in `exceptions.csv` or the test fails.
- Golden cells checked independently against the documents, including 70 lb farthest/nearest zone and lightest/farthest zone per service, card and year.
- Every curated row's quote is found on its cited page.
- Manifest checksums for tracked files; parser unit tests per carrier.

## Test plan
- [x] `uv run pytest -q` — 838 passed (2 warnings = documented USPS zone 9 anomaly)
- [x] `python3 data/fetch_raw.py --verify` — 0 problems
- [x] Each carrier pipeline regenerates its CSVs with no diff

## Known gaps
- UPS fuel defaults are FedEx-derived proxies; UPS announced increases are trade press.
- UPS 2025-26 and 2026-27 peak documents not yet captured (#14). FedEx 2027 surcharge document not archived.

## Summary
- **Q1**: parcel form (weight with ounces, optional dimensions, distance, home or business, speed, delivery area, nonstandard packaging, prefill from the loaded set). Answer first: cheapest total, carrier and service, margin or loss against the flat price. Ranked carriers with base/surcharge/fuel bars; each row expands to every line item with its source citation. - **Q2**: answer first (where USPS stops winning) and one plain sentence per distance band. - Both respond to every global control and state plainly that 2027 is FedEx only.

## Insight from the sample run (2026, commercial, default fuel)
To homes, USPS Ground Advantage wins every weight up to 450 mi. Beyond that it loses only between 27 and 50 lb, and wins again from 51 lb, where UPS and FedEx add Additional Handling for parcels over 50 lb.

=================== PR #24
Model the 90 lb minimum billable weight for large and oversize packages (#18) 2026-10-23T10:57:22Z
## Summary
- New surcharges column `min_billable_weight_lb`, set on every UPS Large Package and FedEx Oversize row; the 90 lb value is read from the carrier's own sentence, cited per year and checked on its page by `data/tests/test_min_billable.py`.
- UPS and FedEx base rates extended to 150 lb (USPS stays at 70) so the minimum is priced from the carriers' tables; invariants and corner cells follow; 150 new golden cells (90 lb and 150 lb) read independently from the documents. FedEx 2023/2024 PDF footnote marker after the weight handled.
- Engine: when an applied rule carries a minimum, billable weight is raised, the base re-priced and surcharges re-matched; a rule that loses its exclusive group brings no minimum; a minimum above the table makes the service unavailable. The base line item says why.
- Behaviour change: UPS/FedEx parcels with dimensional weight 71–150 lb are now priced instead of unavailable. Actual weight stays capped at 70 lb.

Example: UPS Ground 2026, 20 lb, 98×8×8 in, 700 mi, business — $409.04 → $445.56 (billed at 90 lb).

## Test plan
- [x] Backend 80 passed, 99% coverage
- [x] Data suite: all UPS and FedEx citations pass with raw files present; `ALLOW_MISSING_RAW=1` 664 passed

=================== PR #25
Typical sample mix with expedited prices; shared helpers; empty-state handlers (#22) 2026-10-23T11:17:26Z
## Summary
- **Sample mix** (owner decision in #22): an illustrative home-goods shop, 200 rows / 307 parcels, mostly light ground parcels, 7% bulky (1–2 cu ft), 4% two-to-three day and 1% next day with their own charged price ($19.99 / $39.99). Generator is seeded and has a `--check` mode that prices the mix with the engine.
- At default controls (2026, USPS commercial, \$9.99): USPS \$10.98/parcel vs \$10.67 revenue, margin −\$0.31, 40% of parcels at a loss, concentrated in bulky boxes, 5–20 lb and zones 5–8. UPS/FedEx ~\$31.
- API examples and frontend fixtures regenerated; tests updated from hand-checked new numbers, with modified fixtures where the new data no longer reaches a branch.
- Empty-state buttons use real handlers from App; helpers consolidated into `lib/`, `hooks/`, `components/`, `styles/`; mock fixtures no longer in the production bundle (−25 kB gzip).
- README: views described as built, sample paragraph added, in-progress note removed.

=== #14
UPS announced 2026-27 demand surcharges (from 2026-09-27 for Additional Handling, Large Package and Over Maximum; from 2026-10-25 for Ground Residential and Air, per trade press checked 2026-10-23), and the 2025-26 season update is also missing. The primary UPS PDFs are not in `data/raw/manifest.csv`: ups.com blocks the build host and the Wayback Machine was offline on 2026-10-23.

- [ ] Find the Wayback captures of the UPS demand-surcharge PDFs for both seasons (CDX query on `ups.com/assets/resources/webcontent/en_US/*demand*`).
- [ ] Add manifest rows and fetch with `data/fetch_raw.py --record`.
- [ ] Curate peak rows in `data/pipeline/ups_rules.py` with citations; remove the exception notes for 2025 and 2026.

Refs #2
=== #18
UPS Large Package and FedEx Oversize parcels are billed at a minimum of 90 lb. The surcharge rows note it but the engine prices the base at actual or dimensional weight, so the base of such parcels is understated. Add the minimum as data (a column on the oversize rules, with citation) and apply it in billable weight when an oversize rule matches. Found in the PR #17 review.
=== #22
The current sample (300 parcels) makes the shop look badly mispriced: 22% of parcels are bulky boxes over 1 cu ft and 13% ship expedited, all at the flat \$9.99, so 71% ship at a loss even with USPS. That headline is an artefact of the sample, not an insight.

Decision (owner, 2026-10-23):
- Mostly light ground parcels, a minority of bulky boxes, far fewer expedited.
- Use the optional per-parcel `price_charged` column: expedited parcels carry a higher charged price, as real shops charge more for faster shipping.
- Tune so the default view (2026, USPS commercial, \$9.99 flat) opens near break-even overall, with losses concentrated where they would be in reality (bulky, far zones).
- Keep the label: an illustrative home-goods shop, clearly marked as an example (API `description` and the UI).

Tasks:
- [ ] Update `backend/scripts/make_sample_parcels.py` (fixed seed), regenerate `backend/app/sample_parcels.csv`, document the target and the resulting margin in the script docstring.
- [ ] Regenerate `docs/api-examples/` and the frontend fixtures; update tests that depend on sample-derived numbers.
- [ ] README: describe the sample in one paragraph.

```
  is the step for that weight; rules are then matched again at the raised weight. A minimum
  of a rule that lost its exclusive group (e.g. Additional Handling beaten by Large Package)
  does not apply, and no rule other than UPS Large Package / FedEx Oversize carries one.
- **Peak add-ons.** `peak_additional_handling` applies only when a non-peak rule of
  condition `additional_handling_*` (or the UPS cubic-size additional handling recorded as
  `nonstandard_volume`) applied to the same parcel; `peak_oversize` only when an `oversize`
  rule applied. Both carry no thresholds of their own.

Peak rules apply only when the dashboard's peak toggle is on. Rules with
`address_type` `residential` or `commercial` apply only to parcels of that kind.
Delivery area rules apply when the parcel's `delivery_area` is `das` or
`extended`. `additional_handling_packaging` rules apply when the parcel is
flagged `nonstandard_packaging` (UPS and FedEx: non-corrugated or cylindrical
packaging; USPS from 2026: tubes or rolls, cans, wooden or metal boxes, liquids
in glass).

**Tool call:** `Bash` <sub>toolu_014iZcQaKSBni8Wb9HjMptwv</sub>

- `command`:

```
cd ~/parcel-rates-issue-28 && cat >> docs/DECISIONS.md <<'EOF'

## 2026-10-23 — Data collection is fully scripted (issue #13)

- Every step of data collection, including finding the source URLs, must run
  from committed scripts without an LLM or agent, so the data can be rebuilt
  on another machine or refreshed next year. Triggered by the owner asking
  where the download scripts were: the survey agents had fetched with one-off
  calls and only a post-hoc fetcher existed.
- `data/fetch_raw.py` restores every file in `data/raw/manifest.csv` with
  checksums. Scripting the discovery step (Wayback lookups, file-name
  resolution) is tracked in #13 and was left as a bonus by the owner.

## 2026-10-23 — Schema amendments before the parsers (issue #2, PR #15)

The owner reviewed the draft `docs/data/SCHEMA.md` and asked for these changes
before any parser agent was dispatched. From this point every big step
(dispatching agents, merging) waits for the owner's OK.

- **Rate cards.** A `rate_card` column on every base-rate row. USPS keeps
  `retail` and `commercial`, and the dashboard defaults to `commercial`,
  because small online sellers buy postage through shipping software at
  commercial prices and almost never pay counter prices; retail stays
  selectable. UPS Daily Rates and FedEx Standard List Rates are stored as
  `list`, not `retail`: they are the carriers' published prices, which is what
  a shop without a contract pays, and "retail" would suggest a counter price.
  `services.csv` explains each card in one line per carrier. The default is
  explained in the README.
- **Exceptions are warnings, not silent fixes.** Alaska and Hawaii columns,
  rounding artefacts and other anomalies in the carriers' tables are kept as
  printed and listed in `exceptions.csv`; the invariant tests fail on any
  anomaly that is not in that allowlist.
- **Rules that change over time.** The owner asked to check every variable
  that moves a price across years. Result: dimensional-weight rules carry
  `effective_from` / `effective_to` (USPS divisor 166, then 139 from
  2026-07-12), and every price-moving rule change is listed in
  `docs/data/RULE_CHANGES.md`.
- **Surcharge rows** gained `address_type` (carriers price delivery-area and
  large-package surcharges differently for homes and businesses), a width
  threshold column, and a packaging-handling condition with a matching
  `nonstandard_packaging` column on the parcel CSV.
- **Fuel** is applied to the base plus the surcharges flagged `fuel_applies`,
  with tests that check that basis.
- **Golden cells** include, per carrier, card and year, the heaviest weight in
  the farthest and nearest zone and the lightest weight in the farthest zone,
  because column and page shifts in the PDFs break exactly those cells.
- **Curated rules stay diffable.** Surcharge and rule tables are Python
  literals with verbatim quotes, written to committed CSVs, and the
  quote-on-cited-page test runs against those CSVs. Where a carrier rule was
  not found, the note says "not found in the current source" rather than "no
  such surcharge" (for example UPS peak surcharges not yet announced, #14).

## 2026-10-23 — Zone bands from the Domestic Mail Manual (issue #2)

- The brief assumed USPS zones 1 and 2 share a 0–50 mile band and that UPS
  and FedEx zone n equals USPS zone n+1. The USPS pipeline agent fetched DMM
  608.9.4.2, which shows zone 1 up to about 50 miles and zone 2 up to about
  150 as separate bands.
- `zone_from_distance.csv` now follows the DMM for USPS. UPS and FedEx publish
  zones only by ZIP pair, so their bands are derived: zone 2 = 0–150 miles
  (USPS zones 1 and 2 together), zone n = the USPS zone n band for n ≥ 3. The
  derivation is written into the table's `notes` column with the DMM quote.

## 2026-10-23 — Delivery area is a per-parcel opt-in (issue #2, #3)

- Delivery-area and extended-area surcharges are triggered by ZIP-code lists.
  The dashboard works from distance, not ZIP pairs, so it cannot look the area
  up. Instead the parcel CSV has an optional `delivery_area` column (`none`,
  `das`, `extended`) and the quote form has the same control.
- Alternative rejected: applying an average rural share to every parcel,
  which would hide a surcharge that is all or nothing for a given address.
  Real ZIP-to-ZIP zones and area lookup are the bonus in #9.

## 2026-10-23 — Engine rules and the API contract (issues #3–#6, PRs #16, #17)

- One written contract (`docs/API.md`) before backend and frontend work
  started in parallel, so the two could be built by separate agents.
- Threshold semantics fixed in the schema during the engine work: weight
  bands are inclusive on billable weight, single weight thresholds strict on
  actual weight, dimension thresholds strict, `effective_to` inclusive. The
  USPS and UPS rule windows were corrected to match.
- Merging policy: every pull request is reviewed by a review agent and fixed
  before it is merged, with a merge commit.
- Local development uses port 8787 and the compose stack 8088, because 8000
  and 8001 are taken on the development machine (PRs #19, #20).

## 2026-10-23 — Model switch

- The main session and all subagents moved to Opus 5.5 (1M context) on
  2026-10-23 at 08:29, to stay within usage limits for the rest of the build.
  Earlier work, including the data survey, ran on the previous default.

## 2026-10-23 — 90 lb minimum billable weight and base rates to 150 lb (issue #18, PR #24)

- Found in the PR #17 review: UPS Large Package and FedEx Oversize parcels are
  billed at a minimum of 90 lb, but the engine priced the base at actual or
  dimensional weight, understating it.
- The minimum is data, not code: a `min_billable_weight_lb` column on those
  surcharge rows, cited per year and checked on its page. To price it from
  the carriers' own tables, UPS and FedEx base rates are parsed to 150 lb
  (USPS stays at 70). Actual weight stays capped at 70 lb; parcels whose
  dimensional weight is 71–150 lb are now priced instead of unavailable.

## 2026-10-23 — FedEx 2027 stays as a forward year, flagged

- FedEx 2027 base rates are published and parsed, but its surcharge-change
  document is not archived, so only the Home Delivery residential charge and
  the January peak rows exist. Rather than drop the year, it stays selectable
  and the UI states that 2027 is FedEx only and that its totals understate
  surcharges (Q1, Q2, Q4 notes; README limitations).

## 2026-10-23 — Typical sample mix (issue #22, PR #25)

- The first sample (300 parcels, 22 % bulky, 13 % expedited, all at the flat
  $9.99) showed 71 % of parcels shipped at a loss even with USPS. That was an
  artefact of the sample, not an insight.
- Owner decision: an illustrative home-goods shop with mostly light ground
  parcels, 7 % bulky, 4 % two-to-three day and 1 % next day, and the expedited
  parcels carrying their own `price_charged` ($19.99 / $39.99), as real shops
  charge more for speed. The default view now opens near break-even (USPS
  margin −$0.31 per parcel, 40 % of parcels at a loss), with the losses in
  bulky boxes, 5–20 lb and far zones. The label "illustrative example" is kept
  in the API and the UI.

## 2026-10-23 — End-to-end tests and screenshots (issue #8, PR #26)

- Playwright pinned to 1.49 so it runs on Node 18; axe scans fail on serious
  or critical violations and now pass at every level.
- Screenshots that passed the UX review are added to the README, at the
  owner's request; remaining UX items are tracked in #27.

## Decisions changed after reviewing agent output

| Decision | What the agent produced | What changed, and why | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| Zone bands | My brief told the agents UPS/FedEx zone n = USPS zone n+1 | The USPS agent's DMM reading showed zones 1 and 2 are separate; bands rebuilt from the DMM | Agent log 2026-10-23 08:44; PR #15 |
| Rate-card naming | Draft schema labelled UPS and FedEx published prices `retail` | Owner: `list` for UPS and FedEx, `retail`/`commercial` for USPS, default commercial | Agent log 2026-10-23 08:10 and 08:18; PR #15 |

Below are the stack options, my recommendation per item, and the questions I need answered before writing `docs/DECISIONS.md`.  ## Stack options  **1. Backend and data layer** - (a) **FastAPI + pandas over committed CSV/Parquet rate tables, loaded in memory (recommended).** The rate cards are a few thousand rows. No database, no migrations, trivial Docker. - (b) FastAPI + DuckDB. Nice SQL for aggregations over uploaded parcel sets, one more dependency. - (c) FastAPI + SQLite. Rate cards.** A `rate_card` column (`retail` or `commercial`) was added to base rates, surcharges and announced increases, and `services.csv` lists which cards each service publishes. USPS gets both, parsed from the same CSV zips, which contain the commercial tables alongside the retail ones. UPS Daily Rates and FedEx Standard List Rates are stored as `retail` since that is what a shipper without a contract pays. The dashboard defaults USPS to commercial with a control to switch, and the schema states that the README must explain why.  **2. Exceptions as warnings.** The invariant checks (full zone and weight coverage, prices non-decreasing with weight and zone, no float artifacts, every source file in the manifest) no longer just pass or fail. Each anomaly must appear in a per-carrier `exceptions.csv` with a kind such as `ak_hi_zone`, `rounding_artifact`, `non_monotonic` or `page_shift`, a note on how the parser treats it, and a source reference. A listed anomaly produces a warning. An unlisted one fails the test, so nothing odd can go undocumented. Alaska and Hawaii columns are excluded from the dashboard and recorded there explicitly.  **3. Fuel tests.** The engine applies the fuel percent to the base rate plus every applied surcharge flagged `fuel_applies`, and to nothing else. data/pipeline/common.py:129:        raise FileNotFoundError(f"{relative} is missing; run `python3 data/fetch_raw.py`")
"""Every curated row cites a file, a location and a verbatim quote containing the
amount. This test opens the cited location and checks the quote is really there.
Rows whose raw file has not been fetched are skipped with a message."""
from __future__ import annotations

import os

import pytest

from data.pipeline.common import (
    ADDRESS_TYPES, CARRIERS, COLUMNS, CURATED_TABLES, SURCHARGE_CONDITIONS, normalize_text,
    processed_path, read_table, source_text,
)

AMOUNT_COLUMN = {
    "surcharges": "amount",
    "fuel_surcharge": "default_percent",
    "announced_increase": "percent",
    "dim_weight": "divisor",
}


def curated_rows():
    for carrier in CARRIERS:
        for table in CURATED_TABLES:
            for index, row in enumerate(read_table(processed_path(carrier, table))):
                yield pytest.param(carrier, table, row, id=f"{carrier}-{table}-{index}")
    for index, row in enumerate(read_table(processed_path("shared", "zone_from_distance"))):
        yield pytest.param("shared", "zone_from_distance", row, id=f"zone-{index}")


def amount_variants(value: str) -> set[str]:
    """'6.55' may be printed as '$6.55' or '6.55'; '5.9' as '5.9%'; '166' as '166'."""
    value = value.strip().lstrip("-")  # a decrease is quoted as "decrease 3.2 percent"
    variants = {value}
    try:
        number = float(value)
        if number == int(number):
            variants.add(str(int(number)))
        variants.add(f"{number:.2f}")
        variants.add(f"{number:,.2f}")
    except ValueError:
        pass
    return {v.lower() for v in variants}


@pytest.mark.parametrize("owner,table,row", list(curated_rows()))
def test_quote_is_in_cited_source(owner, table, row):
    assert list(row.keys()) == COLUMNS[table], f"{table} columns differ from schema"
    if table == "surcharges":
        assert row["condition"] in SURCHARGE_CONDITIONS, row["condition"]
        assert row["address_type"] in ADDRESS_TYPES, f"address_type must be one of {ADDRESS_TYPES}: {row['surcharge_id']}"
        if row["condition"] == "additional_handling_width":
            assert row["min_width_in"], f"width rule without min_width_in: {row['surcharge_id']}"
    quote = row["source_quote"]
    assert quote.strip(), f"{table}: empty source_quote"
    amount = row.get(AMOUNT_COLUMN.get(table, ""), "")
    if amount:
        assert any(v in quote.lower() for v in amount_variants(amount)), f"quote does not contain the amount {amount}: {quote}"
    ref = row.get("source_page") or row.get("source_ref") or None
    try:
        text = source_text(row["source_file"], ref)
    except FileNotFoundError as error:
        if os.environ.get("ALLOW_MISSING_RAW") == "1":
            pytest.skip(str(error))
        pytest.fail(f"{error}. Run `python3 data/fetch_raw.py`, or set ALLOW_MISSING_RAW=1 to skip on purpose.")
    assert normalize_text(quote) in normalize_text(text), (
        f"quote not found at {row['source_file']} [{ref}]: {quote[:120]}"
    )
"""The minimum billable weight of UPS Large Packages and FedEx Oversize packages is
data with a citation: each year's sentence must be on the cited page, the weight is
read from it, and every oversize row of that year carries that weight and quote."""
from __future__ import annotations

import os

import pytest

from data.pipeline import fedex_rules, ups_rules
from data.pipeline.common import min_billable_lb, normalize_text, processed_path, read_table, source_text

CITATIONS = {
    "ups": {year: {**spec, "file": ups_rules.GUIDES[year]["file"]} for year, spec in ups_rules.LPS_MIN_BILLABLE.items()},
    "fedex": fedex_rules.OVERSIZE_MIN_BILLABLE,
}
CASES = [pytest.param(owner, year, spec, id=f"{owner}-{year}")
         for owner, by_year in CITATIONS.items() for year, spec in by_year.items()]


@pytest.mark.parametrize("owner,year,spec", CASES)
def test_minimum_sentence_is_on_the_cited_page(owner, year, spec):
    try:
        text = normalize_text(source_text(spec["file"], spec["page"]))
    except FileNotFoundError as error:
        if os.environ.get("ALLOW_MISSING_RAW") == "1":
            pytest.skip(str(error))
        pytest.fail(f"{error}. Run `python3 data/fetch_raw.py`, or set ALLOW_MISSING_RAW=1 to skip on purpose.")
    for fragment in spec["quotes"]:
        assert normalize_text(fragment) in text, f"not on {spec['file']} p.{spec['page']}: {fragment}"
    assert min_billable_lb(spec["quotes"][-1]) == "90"


@pytest.mark.parametrize("owner", list(CITATIONS))
def test_every_oversize_row_carries_the_cited_minimum(owner):
    rows = read_table(processed_path(owner, "surcharges"))
    oversize = [r for r in rows if r["condition"] == "oversize"]
    assert {int(r["year"]) for r in oversize} == set(CITATIONS[owner])
    for row in rows:
        if row["condition"] != "oversize":
            "Nov. 11, 2024-Nov. 17, 2024 16.00% 15.75% $0.448 per lb. 22.00% 25.75% "
            "Nov. 4, 2024-Nov. 10, 2024 16.25% 16.00% $0.452 per lb. 22.25% 26.00% previous fedex freight ...")
    rows = ups.parse_fuel_rows(text)
    assert [r.start.isoformat() for r in rows] == ["2024-11-18", "2024-11-11", "2024-11-04"]
    assert rows[0].percents == ["16.00%", "16.00%", "22.50%", "26.25%"]
    from decimal import Decimal
    assert ups.lower_median([Decimal("16.25"), Decimal("16.00"), Decimal("16.00"), Decimal("16.50")]) == Decimal("16.00")


def test_real_2026_ground_page_when_available():
    pdfplumber = pytest.importorskip("pdfplumber")
    try:
        path = raw_path(ups.rules.GUIDES[2026]["file"])
    except FileNotFoundError as error:

    with pdfplumber.open(path) as pdf:

    zones, excluded = table.header.zone_columns(ups.SERVICES["ups_ground"][1])
    assert table.rows[1][0] == "11.99"

import pytest

from data.pipeline.common import CARRIERS, processed_path, read_table


def carriers_with_output(table: str) -> list[str]:
    return [carrier for carrier in CARRIERS if processed_path(carrier, table).is_file()]

`VITE_API_TARGET` points the dev proxy elsewhere. `http://localhost:5173/?mock=1`
runs the frontend alone on recorded API responses. The API contract is in
[docs/API.md](docs/API.md), with example payloads in `docs/api-examples/`
(regenerated by `uv run --directory backend python scripts/export_api_examples.py`).

## Tests

| Suite | Command | What it covers |
|---|---|---|
| Data | `ALLOW_MISSING_RAW=1 uv run pytest` (repository root) | Schema, parsers against golden cells, table invariants, manifest, citations |
| Data, full | `python3 data/fetch_raw.py && uv run pytest` | Same, with every citation checked against its PDF page |
| Backend | `cd backend && uv run pytest` | Rate engine against hand-computed quotes, rule resolution, analysis and API |
| Frontend | `cd frontend && npm ci && npm test` | Components with Testing Library and the API contract test |
| Frontend types | `cd frontend && npm run typecheck` | |
| End to end | `docker compose up -d --build`, then `cd frontend && npx playwright install chromium && npm run e2e` | Playwright at 1440 × 900 and 375 × 812 (touch): the four questions, global controls, URL state, CSV upload, the phone settings sheet, and axe on every view in light and dark |
| Screenshots | same stack, `cd frontend && npm run e2e:update-screens` | Rewrites `frontend/e2e/screenshots/` (every view, both sizes, light and dark), reviewed in [docs/ux-review.md](docs/ux-review.md) |

Without the PDFs, the citation and PDF parser tests fail with a message
telling you to fetch them; `ALLOW_MISSING_RAW=1` skips them on purpose
instead. The end-to-end suite targets `http://localhost:8088`; set
`E2E_BASE_URL` to run it elsewhere, for example against the dev servers
(`E2E_BASE_URL=http://localhost:5173 npm run e2e`).

## Repository layout

```
backend/            FastAPI app (app/), tests, scripts; Dockerfile
frontend/           Vite + React dashboard; Dockerfile and nginx config
data/raw/           manifest.csv, SOURCES.md per carrier, committed raw files

cd ~/parcel-rates-issue-29 && cat > data/tests/raw.py <<'EOF'
"""Tests that open a raw PDF skip when it has not been fetched, so a fresh clone
is green. PARCEL_RATES_REQUIRE_RAW=1 turns those skips into failures for a full
validation run (after `python3 data/fetch_raw.py`)."""
from __future__ import annotations

import os

import pytest

REQUIRE_RAW_ENV = "PARCEL_RATES_REQUIRE_RAW"


def missing_raw(error: FileNotFoundError) -> None:
    """Skip, or fail when PARCEL_RATES_REQUIRE_RAW=1, for a raw file that is not on disk."""
    if os.environ.get(REQUIRE_RAW_ENV) == "1":
        pytest.fail(f"{error} ({REQUIRE_RAW_ENV}=1)")
    pytest.skip(str(error))
EOF

# Frontend

Vite + React + TypeScript dashboard. Design direction and rules: [DESIGN.md](DESIGN.md).

## Run locally

Node 18.18+ (Docker uses Node 22).

```sh
npm ci
| `npm run e2e:update-screens` | rewrite the review screenshots in `e2e/screenshots/` |

## End-to-end tests

Playwright 1.49 (the last release that runs on Node 18) with two projects:
`desktop` (Chromium, 1440 × 900) and `mobile` (Pixel-like, 375 × 812, touch, 2×).
Specs are in `e2e/`; `e2e/helpers.ts` holds the shared steps (open the app, load
the sample, switch question, open the settings rail or sheet). Waits are on API
responses and visible state, never on fixed timeouts.

## Local development without Docker

Needs Python 3.12 with [uv](https://docs.astral.sh/uv/) and Node 18.18 or
newer (the image uses Node 22).

## Summary
- Node 18 is end-of-life and vitest 3 imports `styleText` from `node:util` (Node 20.12 / 21.7+), so the documented version is now **Node 20.12+**: `engines` in `frontend/package.json` and the lockfile root, `frontend/README.md`, root README; `frontend/.nvmrc` added.
- Playwright stays pinned at 1.49.1, now described as pinned so the reviewed screenshots stay stable rather than for Node 18. Docker is unchanged (Node 22).

default.conf
security-headers.conf
server {
    listen 8080;
    server_name _;
    root /usr/share/nginx/html;
    server_tokens off;

    gzip on;
    gzip_vary on;
    gzip_proxied any;
    gzip_min_length 1024;
    gzip_types text/css application/javascript application/json image/svg+xml text/plain;

    location /api/ {
        include /etc/nginx/snippets/security-headers.conf;
        proxy_pass http://backend:8000;
        proxy_http_version 1.1;
        proxy_set_header Host $host;
        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;
        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-Proto $scheme;
        proxy_read_timeout 60s;
        client_max_body_size 5m;
    }

    # Hashed build assets never change under the same name.
    location /assets/ {
        include /etc/nginx/snippets/security-headers.conf;
        add_header Cache-Control "public, max-age=31536000, immutable" always;
        try_files $uri =404;
    }

    # SPA fallback: unknown paths get index.html.
    location / {
        include /etc/nginx/snippets/security-headers.conf;
        add_header Cache-Control "no-cache" always;
        try_files $uri $uri/ /index.html;
    }
}
# Included in every location, because a location's own add_header discards the
# server-level ones. Local app: no HSTS. The CSP allows the Google Fonts
# stylesheet (index.html) and its font files.
add_header X-Content-Type-Options "nosniff" always;
add_header Referrer-Policy "strict-origin-when-cross-origin" always;
add_header X-Frame-Options "DENY" always;

from __future__ import annotations

import json

import pytest

from app.api import models as m
from app.engine import segments as seg
from app.main import REPO_ROOT, load_sample_parcels

PARCEL = {"weight_lb": 2, "distance_miles": 300, "residential": True, "speed": "ground"}


def _msgs(resp):
    assert resp.status_code == 422, resp.text
    return {tuple(d["loc"]): d["msg"] for d in resp.json()["detail"]}


def test_health(client):
    assert client.get("/api/health").json() == {"status": "ok"}


def test_meta(client, tables):
    body = client.get("/api/meta").json()
    m.MetaResponse.model_validate(body)
    assert body["years"]["fedex"] == tables.years_for("fedex")
    assert body["defaults"]["year"] == 2026
    assert set(body["defaults"]["as_of"]) == {"usps", "ups", "fedex"}
    assert any(s["rate_card_notes"] for s in body["services"])
    assert any(f["is_proxy"] for f in body["fuel_defaults"] if f["carrier"] == "ups")
    assert body["segments"]["weight_bands"] == list(m.WeightBand.__args__)
    assert body["segments"]["zone_bands"] == list(m.ZoneBand.__args__)
    assert len(body["limitations"]) >= 6


def test_sample_parcels(client):
    body = client.get("/api/sample-parcels").json()
    assert "illustrative" in body["description"].lower()

"""FastAPI application: loads the rate tables once and serves /api."""

from __future__ import annotations

import csv
import os
from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
from pathlib import Path

from fastapi import FastAPI
from fastapi.exceptions import RequestValidationError
from fastapi.middleware.cors import CORSMiddleware

from app.api.errors import validation_handler
from app.api.models import Parcel
from app.api.routes import router
from app.engine.tables import RateTables, load_tables

REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
DEFAULT_DATA_DIR = REPO_ROOT / "data" / "processed"
SAMPLE_PARCELS_CSV = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "sample_parcels.csv"
DEV_ORIGINS = ["http://localhost:5173"]


def data_dir() -> Path:
    return Path(os.environ.get("DATA_DIR") or DEFAULT_DATA_DIR)


def load_sample_parcels(path: Path = SAMPLE_PARCELS_CSV) -> list[dict]:
    with path.open(newline="", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        rows = list(csv.DictReader(fh))
    parcels = []
    for r in rows:
        clean = {k: v for k, v in r.items() if v not in ("", None)}
        parcels.append(Parcel.model_validate(clean).model_dump(exclude_none=True))
    return parcels


def create_app(tables: RateTables | None = None) -> FastAPI:
    @asynccontextmanager
    async def lifespan(app: FastAPI):
        if getattr(app.state, "tables", None) is None:
            app.state.tables = load_tables(data_dir())
        yield

    app = FastAPI(title="Parcel rates API", version="0.1.0", lifespan=lifespan)
    app.state.tables = tables
    app.state.sample_parcels = load_sample_parcels()
    app.add_middleware(
        CORSMiddleware,
        allow_origins=DEV_ORIGINS,
        allow_methods=["GET", "POST"],
        allow_headers=["*"],
    )
    app.add_exception_handler(RequestValidationError, validation_handler)
    app.include_router(router)
    return app


app = create_app()
from __future__ import annotations

from decimal import ROUND_HALF_UP, Decimal

def client(tables):

def read_csv(rel: str) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
    with (DATA / rel).open(newline="", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        return list(csv.DictReader(fh))


def only(rows: list[dict], **where) -> dict:
    hits = [r for r in rows if all(r[k] == v for k, v in where.items())]
    assert len(hits) == 1, f"expected one row for {where}, got {len(hits)}"
    return hits[0]

"""Interactive API docs under /api, so they are reachable through the nginx proxy.

FastAPI's stock Swagger page loads an unpinned bundle and runs an inline script,
which the nginx Content-Security-Policy blocks. This page pins Swagger UI with
Subresource Integrity and starts it from a same-origin script instead.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from fastapi import APIRouter
from fastapi.responses import HTMLResponse, Response

OPENAPI_URL = "/api/openapi.json"
DOCS_URL = "/api/docs"
SWAGGER_UI = "https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/swagger-ui-dist@5.34.0"
SWAGGER_JS_SRI = "sha384-9Td7MKiIr2S83E1kZyyVi8KdJw/S8CpAvu0llLyMeg0xq/hDGiZJfVn0fV4llCmK"
SWAGGER_CSS_SRI = "sha384-4c3vsE5f+trhFyHrFrLM1fFGUn531NIaC7dcqEWIJdoRQJ5CkJbSuSCwXLpaS1KF"

# The request in docs/api-examples/quote.json, offered by "Try it out".
QUOTE_EXAMPLE = {
    "parcel": {
        "weight_lb": 3.2,
        "length_in": 14,
        "width_in": 12,
        "height_in": 10,
        "distance_miles": 700,
        "residential": True,
        "speed": "ground",
        "delivery_area": "das",
    },
    "controls": {"year": 2026},
}

DOCS_HTML = f"""<!doctype html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
<meta charset="utf-8">
<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">
<title>Parcel rates API</title>
<link rel="stylesheet" href="{SWAGGER_UI}/swagger-ui.css" integrity="{SWAGGER_CSS_SRI}" crossorigin="anonymous">
</head>
<body>
<div id="swagger-ui"></div>
<script src="{SWAGGER_UI}/swagger-ui-bundle.js" integrity="{SWAGGER_JS_SRI}" crossorigin="anonymous"></script>
<script src="{DOCS_URL}/init.js"></script>
</body>
</html>
"""

INIT_JS = f"""SwaggerUIBundle({{
  url: "{OPENAPI_URL}",
  dom_id: "#swagger-ui",
  deepLinking: true,
  tryItOutEnabled: true,
}});
"""

router = APIRouter(include_in_schema=False)


@router.get(DOCS_URL, response_class=HTMLResponse)
def docs() -> str:
    return DOCS_HTML


@router.get(f"{DOCS_URL}/init.js")
def docs_init() -> Response:
    return Response(INIT_JS, media_type="text/javascript")
EOF

cat >> tests/test_api.py <<'EOF'


# ---- interactive docs (issue #33) ------------------------------------------
def test_openapi_schema_under_api(client):
    resp = client.get("/api/openapi.json")
    assert resp.status_code == 200
    assert "/api/quote" in resp.json()["paths"]


def test_docs_page_pins_swagger_with_sri_and_no_inline_script(client):
    from app.api import docs

    resp = client.get("/api/docs")
    assert resp.status_code == 200
    html = resp.text
    assert f'integrity="{docs.SWAGGER_JS_SRI}"' in html
    assert f'integrity="{docs.SWAGGER_CSS_SRI}"' in html
    assert "<script>" not in html  # inline scripts are blocked by the nginx CSP
    init = client.get("/api/docs/init.js")
    assert init.headers["content-type"].startswith("text/javascript")
    assert '"/api/openapi.json"' in init.text
    assert client.get("/docs").status_code == 404


def test_quote_example_matches_docs_and_is_accepted(client):
    from app.api import docs

    recorded = json.loads((REPO_ROOT / "docs/api-examples/quote.json").read_text())
    assert docs.QUOTE_EXAMPLE == recorded["request"]["body"]
    schema = client.get("/api/openapi.json").json()
    body = schema["paths"]["/api/quote"]["post"]["requestBody"]["content"]["application/json"]
    assert docs.QUOTE_EXAMPLE in body["schema"].get("examples", [])
    assert client.post("/api/quote", json=docs.QUOTE_EXAMPLE).status_code == 200
EOF
uv run ruff check app tests 2>&1 | tail -3; uv run pytest -q 2>&1 | tail -4

- `command`:

```
cd ~/parcel-rates-issue-33/frontend && cat > nginx/security-headers-base.conf <<'EOF'
# Headers every response carries; the Content-Security-Policy is added by
# security-headers.conf (the app) or by the /api/docs location.
add_header X-Content-Type-Options "nosniff" always;
add_header Referrer-Policy "strict-origin-when-cross-origin" always;
add_header X-Frame-Options "DENY" always;
EOF
cat > nginx/security-headers.conf <<'EOF'
# Included in every location, because a location's own add_header discards the
# server-level ones. Local app: no HSTS. The CSP allows the Google Fonts
# stylesheet (index.html) and its font files.
include /etc/nginx/snippets/security-headers-base.conf;
add_header Content-Security-Policy "default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' https://fonts.googleapis.com; font-src 'self' https://fonts.gstatic.com; img-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'; object-src 'none'" always;
EOF
python3 - <<'EOF'
p="nginx/default.conf";s=open(p).read()
docs='''    # Interactive API docs: Swagger UI is pinned with Subresource Integrity on
    # jsDelivr (backend/app/api/docs.py), so only these two paths allow it.
    location ~ ^/api/docs(/init\\.js)?$ {
        include /etc/nginx/snippets/security-headers-base.conf;
        add_header Content-Security-Policy "default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' https://cdn.jsdelivr.net; style-src 'self' https://cdn.jsdelivr.net; img-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'; object-src 'none'" always;
        proxy_pass http://backend:8000;
        proxy_http_version 1.1;
        proxy_set_header Host $host;
        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;
        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-Proto $scheme;
    }

    location /api/ {'''
s=s.replace("    location /api/ {",docs,1)
open(p,"w").write(s)
p="Dockerfile";s=open(p).read()
s=s.replace("COPY nginx/security-headers.conf /etc/nginx/snippets/security-headers.conf\n","COPY nginx/security-headers.conf nginx/security-headers-base.conf /etc/nginx/snippets/\n")
open(p,"w").write(s)
EOF

# API contract

The FastAPI backend (`backend/`) serves JSON under `/api`. The frontend talks to
it only through these endpoints. Types below are the Pydantic models
(`backend/app/api/models.py`) and the TypeScript types
(`frontend/src/api/types.ts`), which must stay in sync; a contract test in each
side checks the example payloads in `docs/api-examples/`.

Money is a number in USD rounded to cents. Weights in pounds, lengths in inches,
distances in miles. Years are price-list labels (see `docs/data/SCHEMA.md`).

## Shared types

git add -A && git commit -qm "feat: interactive API docs under /api, allowed by a scoped CSP (#33)" && git push -qu origin issue-33-api-docs 2>&1|tail -1; gh pr create --title "Interactive API docs under /api (#33)" --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## Summary
- `GET /api/docs` (Swagger UI) and `GET /api/openapi.json` now work directly and through nginx; FastAPI's root `/docs` and `/redoc` are off.
- The stock FastAPI page would be blocked by the app's CSP (`script-src 'self'`, inline bootstrap script). `backend/app/api/docs.py` serves its own page instead: Swagger UI **5.34.0 pinned with SRI** (sha384) from jsDelivr and started by a same-origin `/api/docs/init.js`, so no inline script and no `'unsafe-inline'`.
- nginx: header snippet split into `security-headers-base.conf` + the app CSP; a `location` for exactly `/api/docs` and `/api/docs/init.js` adds a CSP that allows `https://cdn.jsdelivr.net` for scripts and styles. Every other path keeps the existing CSP.
- `POST /api/quote` carries the request from `docs/api-examples/quote.json` as its OpenAPI example, so "Try it out" is prefilled; a test keeps the two equal.
- README and `docs/API.md` point to the docs URL; note that the docs page needs network for the CDN (the dashboard does not).

- [x] Backend: 83 passed (3 new: schema under /api, page has SRI and no inline script, example equals docs/api-examples and returns 200)

Closes #33

EOF

  const id = useId();
  const c = data.carriers.find((x) => x.carrier === pick)!;
  const last = c.years[c.years.length - 1]!;
  const def = defaultIncrease(c);
  const value = increase ?? def ?? 0;
  const projected = c.projected;
  const local = Math.round(last.cost_per_parcel * (1 + value / 100) * 100) / 100;
  const shown = projected && projected.increase_percent === value ? projected.cost_per_parcel : local;
  return (

      <h3 id={`${id}-t`} className="q-block__title">
        Next year, {last.year + 1}
      </h3>
      <div className="next-year__row">
        <div className="next-year__control">
          <div className="next-year__head">
            <label htmlFor={id}>Increase for {last.year + 1}</label>
            <output htmlFor={id} className="next-year__value">
              {signedPct(value)}
            </output>
          </div>
          <input
            id={id}
            type="range"
            min={INCREASE_MIN}
            max={INCREASE_MAX}
            step={0.1}
            value={value}
            aria-describedby={`${id}-hint`}
            aria-valuetext={signedPct(value)}
            onChange={(e) => onIncrease(Number(e.target.value))}
          />
          <p id={`${id}-hint`} className="next-year__hint">
            {def !== null ? (
              <>
                Starts at {carrierName(pick)}’s latest announced increase, {signedPct(def)}.{" "}
                {value !== def && (
                  <button type="button" className="btn btn--quiet next-year__reset" onClick={() => onIncrease(def)}>
                    Reset to {signedPct(def)}
                  </button>
                )}
              </>
            ) : (
              "No announced increase recorded to start from."
            )}
          </p>
        </div>
        <p className="next-year__result" data-testid="q4-projection">
          <span className="figure next-year__figure" style={{ color: carrierVar(pick) }}>
            {formatUsd(shown)}
          </span>{" "}
          per parcel with {carrierName(pick)} in {last.year + 1}, from {formatUsd(last.cost_per_parcel)} in {last.year} (
          {signedUsd(shown - last.cost_per_parcel)}). The dotted lines in the chart apply the same increase to every carrier.
        </p>
      </div>
    </section>
  );

import { useState } from "react";
import { CartesianGrid, Line, LineChart, ReferenceLine, ResponsiveContainer, Tooltip, XAxis, YAxis } from "recharts";
import type { Carrier, YearlyResponse } from "../../api/types";
import { useMediaQuery } from "../../hooks/useMediaQuery";

import { formatUsd, signedPct } from "../../lib/format";

const COLOR_VARS = {
  usps: "--carrier-usps",
  ups: "--carrier-ups",
  fedex: "--carrier-fedex",
  muted: "--ink-muted",
  ink: "--ink",
  rule: "--rule",
  sheet: "--sheet",
  paper: "--paper",
  loss: "--loss",
} as const;

/**
 * UPS and FedEx list prices track each other within a few cents, and FedEx is drawn last, so
 * UPS gets a wider line and a larger marker that show around FedEx's where the two overlap.
 */
const UNDERLAY: Partial<Record<Carrier, { width: number; r: number }>> = { ups: { width: 5.5, r: 7 } };

/** Marker shape per carrier, so lines differ without colour. */

/** Pure derivations for the Q4 year-over-year view. */
import type { Carrier, CarrierYears, Driver, Drivers, YearRow, YearlyResponse } from "../../api/types";
import { carrierName } from "../../lib/carriers";
import { conditionLabel, formatUsd, signedPct, signedUsd, tierName } from "../../lib/format";

export type Dimension = keyof Drivers;
export const DIMENSIONS: readonly Dimension[] = ["weight_band", "zone_band", "residential", "speed"];
export const DIMENSION_LABEL: Record<Dimension, string> = {
  weight_band: "Weight",
  zone_band: "Distance",
  residential: "Address",
  speed: "Speed",
};

const round2 = (n: number) => Math.round(n * 100) / 100;

/** Latest year every carrier with data publishes (2026 when FedEx alone has 2027). */
export function referenceYear(r: YearlyResponse): number | null {
  const sets = r.carriers.filter((c) => c.years.length > 0).map((c) => new Set(c.years.map((y) => y.year)));
  if (sets.length === 0) return null;
  const common = [...sets[0]!].filter((y) => sets.every((s) => s.has(y)));
  if (common.length) return Math.max(...common);
  return Math.max(...r.carriers.flatMap((c) => c.years.map((y) => y.year)));
}

export const rowFor = (c: CarrierYears, year: number) => c.years.find((y) => y.year === year) ?? null;

/** The carrier the owner would pick: cheapest per parcel in the reference year. */

/** Default for the next-year slider: the pick carrier's latest announced increase. */
export function defaultIncrease(c: CarrierYears | null): number | null {
  if (!c) return null;
  for (let i = c.years.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const p = c.years[i]!.announced_increase.percent;
    if (p !== null) return p;
  }
  return null;
}

**Tool call:** `Bash` <sub>toolu_014JPQMTR4fXRkJpnEqxYyoR</sub>

- `command`:

```
S=/tmp/claude-1000/-home-aivuk-parcel-rates/c1ff8718-9cd7-4b4d-8460-8656e3d95ba6/scratchpad; export PATH=$S/node-v20.12.2-linux-x64/bin:$PATH; cd ~/parcel-rates-issue-34/frontend && sed -i 's/FedEx 2027 is published prices, not a projection/FedEx 2027 is a published price list, not a projection/g' src/views/yearly/YearlyView.test.tsx && python3 - <<'EOF'
p="src/views/yearly/logic.ts";s=open(p).read()
anchor="/** Default for the next-year slider: the pick carrier's latest announced increase. */"
helper='''const joinAnd = (words: string[]) =>
  words.length < 2 ? (words[0] ?? "") : `${words.slice(0, -1).join(", ")} and ${words[words.length - 1]}`;

/**
 * Projections start after each carrier's last published year, so they can land on different
 * years (FedEx publishes 2027 before the others). Says so, and names the published years that
 * sit where the other carriers are projected.
 */
export function projectionSentence(carriers: CarrierYears[]): string | null {
  const projected = carriers.filter((c) => c.projected !== null);
  if (projected.length === 0) return null;
  const base = "Each projection starts after that carrier’s last published year";
  const years = [...new Set(projected.map((c) => c.projected!.year))].sort((a, b) => a - b);
  if (years.length === 1) return `${base}.`;
  const first = years[0]!;
  const groups = years.map((y, i) => {
    const names = joinAnd(projected.filter((c) => c.projected!.year === y).map((c) => carrierName(c.carrier)));
    return i === 0 ? `${names} project to ${y}` : `${names} to ${y}`;
  });
  const published = projected
    .filter((c) => c.projected!.year > first)
    .map((c) => {
      const ahead = c.years.map((r) => r.year).filter((y) => y >= first);
      const what = ahead.length === 1 ? "is a published price list" : "are published price lists";
      return `${carrierName(c.carrier)} ${joinAnd(ahead.map(String))} ${what}`;
    });
  return `${base}: ${joinAnd(groups)}; ${joinAnd(published)}, not a projection.`;
}

'''

open(p,"w").write(s)

    </>""","""      Dashed lines follow the announced increases from the first year; dotted segments are the projection.
      {projectionNote && <> {projectionNote}</>}
    </>""")
s=s.replace("""  const summary = (
    <>""","""  const projectionNote = projectionSentence(carriers);
  const summary = (
    <>""",1)
open(p,"w").write(s)

# One lint config for data/ and backend/ (backend/pyproject.toml has no [tool.ruff],
# so ruff uses this one). Run with `uvx ruff check backend data`.
[tool.ruff]
target-version = "py312"
line-length = 120

[tool.ruff.lint]
select = ["E4", "E7", "E9", "F", "I", "B"]
# zip(strict=True) would change behaviour on uneven inputs; reviewed case by case instead.
ignore = ["B905"]

[tool.ruff.lint.flake8-bugbear]
extend-immutable-calls = ["fastapi.Depends", "fastapi.Body", "fastapi.Query"]

[tool.ruff.lint.isort]
known-first-party = ["app", "data"]
EOF

def values_of(rows: list[dict], table: str) -> list[str]:
    ordered = sorted(rows, key=lambda r: r.get("effective_from", ""))
    out = []
    for row in ordered:
        text = ", ".join(f"{c} {row[c]}" for c in VALUE_COLUMNS[table] if row.get(c, ""))
        window = row.get("effective_from", "")
        if len(ordered) > 1 and window:
            text = f"from {window}: {text}"
        out.append(text)
    return out

- `command`:

```
mkdir -p ~/parcel-rates-issue-32/.github/workflows && cat > ~/parcel-rates-issue-32/.github/workflows/ci.yml <<'EOF'
name: CI

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
  pull_request:
  workflow_dispatch:

permissions:
  contents: read

concurrency:
  group: ci-${{ github.ref }}
  cancel-in-progress: true

jobs:
  python:
    name: Lint and Python tests
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
      - uses: astral-sh/setup-uv@v10
        with:
          python-version: "3.12"
      - name: Lint
        run: uvx ruff check backend data
      - name: Data tests (PDF citations skipped, see README "Tests")
        run: uv run pytest data/tests
      - name: Backend tests
        working-directory: backend
        run: uv run pytest

  frontend:
    name: Frontend tests and build
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    defaults:
      run:
        working-directory: frontend
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v7
        with:
          node-version: "20.12" # the documented minimum
          cache: npm
          cache-dependency-path: frontend/package-lock.json
      - run: npm ci
      - run: npm test
      - run: npm run typecheck
      - run: npm run build

  docker:
    name: Docker build (reviewer run path)
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
      - run: docker compose build

  data-full:
    name: Data tests with every PDF
    # Fetching from the Wayback Machine can fail for reasons outside the repo;
    # the job reports but does not block. PDFs are cached by manifest checksum,
    # so the archive is only hit when the manifest changes.
    if: github.event_name != 'pull_request'
    continue-on-error: true
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
      - uses: astral-sh/setup-uv@v10
        with:
          python-version: "3.12"
      - uses: actions/cache@v6
        with:
          path: data/raw/**/*.pdf
          key: raw-pdfs-${{ hashFiles('data/raw/manifest.csv') }}
      - name: Fetch raw files
        run: python3 data/fetch_raw.py
      - name: Data tests, missing PDFs fail
        run: uv run pytest data/tests
        env:
          PARCEL_RATES_REQUIRE_RAW: "1"
EOF
cd ~/parcel-rates-issue-32 && python3 -c "import yaml" 2>/dev/null && python3 -c "import yaml;yaml.safe_load(open('.github/workflows/ci.yml'));print('yaml ok')"; uvx actionlint --version >/dev/null 2>&1 && echo has-actionlint; which actionlint
```

All six PRs have been reviewed, the fixes are pushed, and nothing is merged. My first merge (`gh pr merge 36`) was blocked by the permission checker as "merge without review": the reviews ran as local agents, so GitHub shows no review on the PR. I haven't tried to get around it.

**What the reviews found and what I changed:**
- **#35 (DECISIONS.md):**
  - The heading said collection was fully scripted, but finding the source URLs is still manual. That part is still open in #13, and the entry now says so.
  - The README still said the 90 lb minimum was not modelled, which stopped being true with #24. Fixed.
  - A log timestamp now matches the exported log (13:23).
- **#38 (Q4 caption):** a carrier with no published years in the gap would have produced a broken sentence ("FedEx  are published…"). That case now names no years, and there's a test for it (196 tests pass).
- **#40 (#29, new PR):** `missing_raw` is now typed `NoReturn`, and a test was renamed. With the PDFs fetched (76 of 77):
  - without the flag: 861 passed, 8 skipped
  - with `PARCEL_RATES_REQUIRE_RAW=1`: 861 passed, 8 failed, all on the UPS file the Wayback Machine refuses, the same one PR #17 reported
- **#36 (Node version):** the README now gives a reason I can back up for keeping Playwright at 1.49.
- **#37 (API docs):** the integrity hashes and the page-only security policy check out; I added a comment that the page templates must never include request data.
- **#39 (CI):** the reviewer's two findings come from reading it without #40, which adds the skip default and the flag. On CI, lint, frontend and Docker pass; the data step shows the 600 missing-PDF failures that #40 fixes.

**To finish, either:**
- merge them yourself in this order: #35, #40, #36, #37, #38. Then I rebase #39, wait for green CI, and you merge it; or
- add a Bash permission rule for `gh pr merge` and tell me to continue, and I'll do the merges and the #39 rebase.

```
/** Pure derivations for the Q4 year-over-year view. */
import type { Carrier, CarrierYears, Driver, Drivers, YearRow, YearlyResponse } from "../../api/types";
import { carrierName } from "../../lib/carriers";
import { conditionLabel, formatUsd, signedPct, signedUsd, tierName } from "../../lib/format";

export type Dimension = keyof Drivers;
export const DIMENSIONS: readonly Dimension[] = ["weight_band", "zone_band", "residential", "speed"];
export const DIMENSION_LABEL: Record<Dimension, string> = {
  weight_band: "Weight",
  zone_band: "Distance",
  residential: "Address",
  speed: "Speed",
};

const round2 = (n: number) => Math.round(n * 100) / 100;

/** Latest year every carrier with data publishes (2026 when FedEx alone has 2027). */
export function referenceYear(r: YearlyResponse): number | null {
  const sets = r.carriers.filter((c) => c.years.length > 0).map((c) => new Set(c.years.map((y) => y.year)));
  if (sets.length === 0) return null;
  const common = [...sets[0]!].filter((y) => sets.every((s) => s.has(y)));
  if (common.length) return Math.max(...common);
  return Math.max(...r.carriers.flatMap((c) => c.years.map((y) => y.year)));
}

export const rowFor = (c: CarrierYears, year: number) => c.years.find((y) => y.year === year) ?? null;

/** The carrier the owner would pick: cheapest per parcel in the reference year. */
export function pickCarrier(r: YearlyResponse, ref: number): CarrierYears | null {
  let best: CarrierYears | null = null;
  let bestCost = Infinity;
  for (const c of r.carriers) {
    const row = rowFor(c, ref);
    if (row && c.parcels_used > 0 && row.cost_per_parcel < bestCost) {
      best = c;
      bestCost = row.cost_per_parcel;
    }
  }
  return best;
}

export interface PeriodChange {
  fromYear: number;
  toYear: number;
  from: number;
  to: number;
  dollars: number;
  percent: number;
  /** Announced increases compounded over the same years; null when a year has none. */
  announcedPercent: number | null;
}

/** Change in cost per parcel from the carrier's first year to `toYear`, against the compounded announcements. */
export function periodChange(c: CarrierYears, toYear: number): PeriodChange | null {
  const first = c.years[0];
  const last = rowFor(c, toYear);
  if (!first || !last || last.year <= first.year || first.cost_per_parcel === 0) return null;
  let factor: number | null = 1;
  for (const y of c.years) {
    if (y.year <= first.year || y.year > toYear) continue;
    const p = y.announced_increase.percent;
    factor = p === null || factor === null ? null : factor * (1 + p / 100);
  }
  return {
    fromYear: first.year,
    toYear,
    from: first.cost_per_parcel,
    to: last.cost_per_parcel,
    dollars: round2(last.cost_per_parcel - first.cost_per_parcel),
    percent: ((last.cost_per_parcel - first.cost_per_parcel) / first.cost_per_parcel) * 100,
    announcedPercent: factor === null ? null : (factor - 1) * 100,
  };
}

/** Cost path if every year had risen exactly by the announced increase, from the first year's cost. */
export function announcedPath(c: CarrierYears): Map<number, number> {
  const out = new Map<number, number>();
  const first = c.years[0];
  if (!first) return out;
  let v = first.cost_per_parcel;
  out.set(first.year, v);
  for (const y of c.years.slice(1)) {
    const p = y.announced_increase.percent;
    if (p === null) break;
    v = v * (1 + p / 100);
    out.set(y.year, round2(v));
  }
  return out;
}

const joinAnd = (words: string[]) =>
  words.length < 2 ? (words[0] ?? "") : `${words.slice(0, -1).join(", ")} and ${words[words.length - 1]}`;

/**
 * Projections start after each carrier's last published year, so they can land on different
 * years (FedEx publishes 2027 before the others). Says so, and names the published years that
 * sit where the other carriers are projected.
 */
export function projectionSentence(carriers: CarrierYears[]): string | null {
  const projected = carriers.filter((c) => c.projected !== null);
  if (projected.length === 0) return null;
  const base = "Each projection starts after that carrier’s last published year";
  const years = [...new Set(projected.map((c) => c.projected!.year))].sort((a, b) => a - b);
  if (years.length === 1) return `${base}.`;
  const first = years[0]!;
  const groups = years.map((y, i) => {
    const names = joinAnd(projected.filter((c) => c.projected!.year === y).map((c) => carrierName(c.carrier)));
    return i === 0 ? `${names} project to ${y}` : `${names} to ${y}`;
  });
  const published = projected
    .filter((c) => c.projected!.year > first)
    .map((c) => {
      const ahead = c.years.map((r) => r.year).filter((y) => y >= first);
      const what = ahead.length === 1 ? "is a published price list" : "are published price lists";
      return `${carrierName(c.carrier)} ${joinAnd(ahead.map(String))} ${what}`;
    });
  return `${base}: ${joinAnd(groups)}; ${joinAnd(published)}, not a projection.`;
}

/** Default for the next-year slider: the pick carrier's latest announced increase. */
export function defaultIncrease(c: CarrierYears | null): number | null {
  if (!c) return null;
  for (let i = c.years.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const p = c.years[i]!.announced_increase.percent;
    if (p !== null) return p;
  }
  return null;
}

/* ---------- fuel ---------- */

export interface FuelStep {
  carrier: Carrier;
  fromYear: number;
  toYear: number;
  change: number;
  fuelChange: number;
  /** fuel change as a share of the total change (can exceed 1 or be negative) */
  share: number | null;
  groundFrom: number | null;
  groundTo: number | null;
}

export function fuelStep(c: CarrierYears, prev: YearRow, cur: YearRow): FuelStep | null {
  if (!cur.fuel || !prev.fuel || !cur.change_vs_previous) return null;
  const change = cur.change_vs_previous.dollars_per_parcel;
  const fuelChange = round2(cur.summary.split.fuel.per_parcel - prev.summary.split.fuel.per_parcel);
  return {
    carrier: c.carrier,
    fromYear: prev.year,
    toYear: cur.year,
    change,
    fuelChange,
    share: change !== 0 ? fuelChange / change : null,
    groundFrom: prev.fuel.ground.applied_percent,
    groundTo: cur.fuel.ground.applied_percent,
  };
}

/**
 * The year step where the carrier's cost rose most, with its fuel part; null when no step is a
 * rise (the "how much of the rise is fuel" question does not apply) or the carrier has no fuel.
 */
export function biggestFuelStep(c: CarrierYears, upTo?: number): FuelStep | null {
  let best: FuelStep | null = null;
  for (let i = 1; i < c.years.length; i++) {
    const cur = c.years[i]!;
    if (upTo !== undefined && cur.year > upTo) break;
    const s = fuelStep(c, c.years[i - 1]!, cur);
    if (s && s.change > 0 && (!best || s.change > best.change)) best = s;
  }
  return best;
}

export function fuelSentence(s: FuelStep): string {
  const name = carrierName(s.carrier);
  const pct = s.share !== null && s.change > 0 && s.fuelChange > 0 ? ` (${Math.round(s.share * 100)}% of it)` : "";
  const rates =
    s.groundFrom !== null && s.groundTo !== null && s.groundFrom !== s.groundTo
      ? `; the ground fuel surcharge went from ${s.groundFrom}% to ${s.groundTo}%`
      : "";
  return `${name} ${s.fromYear}→${s.toYear}: ${signedUsd(s.change)} per parcel, of which fuel ${signedUsd(s.fuelChange)}${pct}${rates}.`;
}

/* ---------- drivers ---------- */

export function sortedDrivers(list: Driver[]): Driver[] {
  return [...list].sort((a, b) => Math.abs(b.change_per_parcel) - Math.abs(a.change_per_parcel) || a.segment.localeCompare(b.segment));
}

export function segmentLabel(dim: Dimension, segment: string): string {
  switch (dim) {
    case "weight_band":
      return `${segment} lb`;
    case "zone_band":
      return `Zones ${segment}`;
    case "residential":
      return segment === "residential" ? "Homes" : segment === "business" ? "Businesses" : segment;
    case "speed":
      return tierName(segment);
  }
}

function segmentPhrase(dim: Dimension, segment: string): string {
  switch (dim) {
    case "weight_band":
      return `parcels of ${segment} lb`;
    case "zone_band":
      return `parcels to zones ${segment}`;
    case "residential":
      return segment === "residential" ? "deliveries to homes" : "deliveries to businesses";
    case "speed":
      return `${tierName(segment).toLowerCase()} parcels`;
  }
}

/** The dimension whose largest single segment explains most of the change. */
export function strongestDimension(d: Drivers): Dimension {
  let best: Dimension = "weight_band";
  let bestV = -1;
  for (const dim of DIMENSIONS) {
    const top = sortedDrivers(d[dim])[0];
    const v = top ? Math.abs(top.change_per_parcel) : 0;
    if (v > bestV) {
      best = dim;
      bestV = v;
    }
  }
  return best;
}

/** Change per parcel *within* the segment: its own parcels' change, independent of its size. */
export function withinChange(d: Driver): number {
  return d.parcels > 0 ? Math.round((d.change_total / d.parcels) * 100) / 100 : 0;
}

/** The segment whose own parcels moved most in the direction of the overall change. */
export function fastestSegment(list: Driver[], overall: number): Driver | null {
  const withParcels = list.filter((d) => d.parcels > 0);
  if (withParcels.length === 0) return null;
  const sign = overall >= 0 ? 1 : -1;
  return withParcels.reduce((best, d) => (sign * withinChange(d) > sign * withinChange(best) ? d : best));
}

export function driverSentence(carrier: Carrier, row: YearRow, dim: Dimension): string | null {
  const ch = row.change_vs_previous;
  const list = row.drivers?.[dim];
  if (!ch || !list || list.length === 0) return null;
  const top = sortedDrivers(list)[0]!;
  const name = carrierName(carrier);
  const span = `${ch.from_year} to ${row.year}`;
  const what = segmentPhrase(dim, top.segment);
  const share = ch.dollars_per_parcel !== 0 ? Math.round((top.change_per_parcel / ch.dollars_per_parcel) * 100) : null;
  const shareText = share !== null && share > 0 ? `, ${share}% of it` : "";
  const direction = top.change_per_parcel >= 0 ? "added" : "took off";
  const first =
    `Largest contributor: ${what} (${top.parcels} of them) ${direction} ${formatUsd(Math.abs(top.change_per_parcel))} per parcel ` +
    `of ${name}'s ${signedUsd(ch.dollars_per_parcel)} change from ${span}${shareText}.`;
  const fast = fastestSegment(list, ch.dollars_per_parcel);
  if (!fast) return first;
  const verb = ch.dollars_per_parcel >= 0 ? "rising" : "falling";
  if (fast.segment === top.segment) return `${first} Its own parcels also changed fastest: ${signedUsd(withinChange(fast))} each.`;
  const fastWhat = segmentPhrase(dim, fast.segment);
  const fastShare = Math.round((fast.parcels / list.reduce((n, d) => n + d.parcels, 0)) * 100);
  return (
    `${first} Fastest ${verb}: ${fastWhat}, ${signedUsd(withinChange(fast))} each, ` +
    `but they are only ${fastShare}% of the parcels (${fast.parcels}), so they move the average less.`
  );
}

/* ---------- warnings ---------- */

const MONTHS = ["Jan", "Feb", "Mar", "Apr", "May", "Jun", "Jul", "Aug", "Sep", "Oct", "Nov", "Dec"];

export function formatDate(iso: string): string {
  const [y, m, d] = iso.split("-").map(Number);
  return `${d} ${MONTHS[(m ?? 1) - 1]} ${y}`;
}

export interface PointNote {
  carrier: Carrier;
  year: number;
  text: string;
  kind: "warning" | "edition";
}

/**
 * Warnings from the response, plus a note when the year's prices took effect mid-year
 * (the backend reports `as_of`, not a warning, for that): a year priced from a mid-year
 * edition is not a January-to-January comparison.
 */
export function pointNotes(r: YearlyResponse): PointNote[] {
  const out: PointNote[] = [];
  for (const c of r.carriers) {
    for (const y of c.years) {
      for (const w of y.warnings) out.push({ carrier: c.carrier, year: y.year, text: readableWarning(w), kind: "warning" });
      if (y.as_of) {
        const [ay, am] = y.as_of.split("-").map(Number);
        if (ay === y.year && am !== undefined && am >= 3) {
          out.push({
            carrier: c.carrier,
            year: y.year,
            kind: "edition",
            text: `Priced from the edition effective ${formatDate(y.as_of)}, published mid-year, so the step into and out of ${y.year} is not January to January.`,
          });
        }
      }
    }
  }
  return out;
}

export const pctText = (n: number) => signedPct(n, 1);

/** "No 2027 rules recorded for: a_b, c; …" with the surcharge ids in the owner's words. */
export function readableWarning(w: string): string {
  const m = /^(No \d{4} rules recorded for): ([^;]+);\s*(.*)$/.exec(w);
  if (!m) return w;
  const kinds = m[2]!.split(",").map((k) => conditionLabel(k.trim()).toLowerCase());
  const rest = m[3]!.replace(/^./, (ch) => ch.toUpperCase());
  return `${m[1]} ${kinds.join(", ")}. ${rest}${/[.!]$/.test(rest) ? "" : "."}`;
}
```
